//! Real operation histories from a complete trusted fixture, including assets.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#122-reproducible-campaigns-and-reachability — only actual fixture traces establish application reachability.
use super::*;
const LIMIT:usize=512;
#[derive(Clone,Debug,Serialize)]
pub struct Step {pub operation:String,pub viewer:String,pub args:BTreeMap<String,Value>,pub result:serde_json::Value}

pub(super) fn viewers(bound:&Bound)->Vec<String>{
    let mut out=BTreeSet::from([VIEWER.into(),OTHER.into(),"account:security-no-role".into(),"service:security".into()]);
    out.extend(bound.personas.iter().map(|p|if p.contains(':'){p.clone()}else{format!("dev:{p}")}));
    fn seats(value:&serde_json::Value,out:&mut BTreeSet<String>){match value{
        serde_json::Value::String(s) if s.starts_with("service:")&&s.len()>8=>{out.insert(s.clone());},
        serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>for x in xs{seats(x,out)},
        serde_json::Value::Object(xs)=>for x in xs.values(){seats(x,out)},_=>{}
    }}
    seats(&serde_json::to_value(&bound.schema).unwrap(),&mut out);
    for p in &bound.programs{seats(&serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap(),&mut out);}
    seats(&serde_json::to_value(&bound.invariants).unwrap(),&mut out);
    out.into_iter().collect()
}
fn actors(bound:&Bound,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>)->Vec<String>{
    let mut out=viewers(bound);
    for (table,rs) in rows{for row in rs{for (c,k) in &bound.schema.tables[table].columns{
        if matches!(k.required(),C::Principal)&&let Ok(Value::Id(p))=row.field(c)&&!out.contains(p){out.push(p.clone());}
    }}}
    out
}
pub(super) fn fixture_cases(args:&BTreeMap<String,ir::ArgType>,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,actors:&[String],out:&mut Campaign)->Vec<BTreeMap<String,Value>>{
    fn values(name:&str,k:&ir::ArgType,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,actors:&[String])->Option<Vec<Value>>{
        Some(match k{
            ir::ArgType::Enum{variants}=>variants.iter().map(|s|Value::String(s.clone())).collect(),
            ir::ArgType::Bool=>vec![Value::Bool(false),Value::Bool(true)],
            ir::ArgType::Time=>vec![Value::Int(NOW-86_400_000),Value::Int(NOW),Value::Int(NOW+86_400_000)],
            ir::ArgType::Principal=>actors.iter().map(|s|Value::Id(s.clone())).collect(),
            ir::ArgType::Ref{table} if rows.get(table).is_some_and(|rs|!rs.is_empty())=>rows[table].iter().filter_map(|r|r.field("id").ok().cloned()).collect(),
            ir::ArgType::Optional(inner)=>{let mut v=vec![Value::Null];v.extend(values(name,inner,rows,actors)?);v},
            _=>vec![arguments(&BTreeMap::from([(name.into(),k.clone())]),0)?.remove(name)?],
        })
    }
    let mut cases=vec![BTreeMap::new()];
    for (name,k) in args{
        let Some(vals)=values(name,k,rows,actors)else{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{name}: history needs a typed asset argument"));return Vec::new();};
        let mut next=Vec::new();
        for n in 0..vals.len() {for c in &cases{
            let mut case=c.clone();case.insert(name.clone(),vals[n%vals.len()].clone());
            if !next.contains(&case){next.push(case);}if next.len()==LIMIT{break;}
        }if next.len()==LIMIT{out.unsupported.insert("history arguments capped at 512 combinations".into());break;}}
        cases=next;
    }cases
}
fn violation(out:&mut Campaign,inv:&Invariant,steps:&[Step],viewer:&str,row:&Value,old:Option<&Value>,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,effect:&str){
    let committed=|steps:&[Step]|steps.iter().filter(|s|s.result.get("state").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)==Some("sent")).count();
    if out.violations.iter().any(|v|v.invariant_site==inv.site.to_string()&&v.viewer==viewer&&v.reachability==Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace&&committed(&v.history)==committed(steps)){return;}
    out.violations.push(Counterexample{reachability:Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace,observations:None,history:steps.to_vec(),invariant_site:inv.site.to_string(),args:BTreeMap::new(),viewer:viewer.into(),operation:steps.last().map(|s|s.operation.clone()).unwrap_or("$fixture.read".into()),effect:effect.into(),row:row.clone(),old:old.cloned(),supporting_rows:initial.clone(),rule_path:"actual authored operation/read from the trusted fixture".into(),scope:"reachable from recorded trusted fixture; finite trace, not an all-history proof".into()});
}
fn typed_disclosure(ty:&crate::types::Ty,data:&Value,table:&str,key:&Value,column:Option<&str>,physical:&Value)->bool{
    use crate::types::Ty;
    let payload=|data:&Value|matches!(data,Value::Row(_))&&data.field("id").ok()==Some(key)&&column.is_none_or(|c|data.field(c).ok().is_some_and(|v|physical.field(c).ok()==Some(v)));
    match ty{
        Ty::Row(t)|Ty::WriteRow(t) if t==table=>payload(data),
        Ty::Optional(inner)=>typed_disclosure(inner,data,table,key,column,physical),
        Ty::Page(t,_) if t==table=>matches!(data,Value::Array(xs) if xs.iter().any(payload)),
        Ty::List(inner,_)|Ty::Collection(inner,_)=>matches!(data,Value::Array(xs) if xs.iter().any(|r|typed_disclosure(inner,r,table,key,column,physical))),
        Ty::Object(fields)|Ty::RowShape(fields)|Ty::RowObject(fields)=>fields.iter().any(|(name,ty)|data.field(name).ok().is_some_and(|v|typed_disclosure(ty,v,table,key,column,physical))),
        _=>false,
    }
}
fn typed_source(ty:&crate::types::Ty,table:&str)->bool{
    use crate::types::Ty;
    match ty{Ty::Row(t)|Ty::WriteRow(t)|Ty::Page(t,_)=>t==table,Ty::Optional(inner)|Ty::List(inner,_)|Ty::Collection(inner,_)=>typed_source(inner,table),Ty::Object(fields)|Ty::RowShape(fields)|Ty::RowObject(fields)=>fields.values().any(|ty|typed_source(ty,table)),_=>false}
}
fn observer_tables(bound:&Bound,table:&str)->BTreeSet<String>{
    let mut out=BTreeSet::from([table.into()]);
    loop{let before=out.len();
        for m in &bound.schema.maintains{if out.contains(&m.source){out.insert(m.target.clone());if let Some(owner)=&m.private{out.insert(owner.table.clone());}}}
        for (name,t) in &bound.schema.tables{if t.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|edge|out.contains(&edge.parent))||[&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete].iter().any(|r|kernel::dependencies(r).iter().any(|t|out.contains(t))){out.insert(name.clone());}}
        if out.len()==before{return out;}
    }
}
fn reserve_observers(out:&mut Campaign,calls:usize)->bool{
    if out.history_observer_calls+calls>32768{out.unsupported.insert("history read observers capped at 32768 calls; remaining disclosure combinations not covered".into());false}else{out.history_observer_calls+=calls;true}
}
fn observe(bound:&Bound,e:&mut Engine,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,actors:&[String],steps:&[Step],out:&mut Campaign){
    if !reserve_observers(out,0)||out.history_observer_calls>=32768{return;}
    let queries=bound.programs.iter().filter(|bp|bp.program.kind==ProgramKind::Query&&!bp.program.native).filter(|bp|{
        let json=serde_json::to_string(&bp.program).unwrap();
        if ["NewId","Emit","JobAs","JobQuery"].iter().any(|tag|json.contains(tag)){
            out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: generated-id/opaque history observer needs disclosure provenance",bp.program.name));false
        }else{true}
    }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="read"){
        let direct=inv.policy.as_ref().is_some_and(|p|kernel::implies(&super::super::columns::admission(&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table],inv.column.as_deref()),p)==Ok(true));
        let indirect=bound.schema.maintains.iter().any(|m|m.source==inv.table)||bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|[&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete].iter().any(|r|kernel::dependencies(r).contains(&inv.table)));
        if direct&&!indirect{continue;}

        let observing=observer_tables(bound,&inv.table);
        for row in rows.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){'actors:for viewer in actors{
            let committed=steps.iter().filter(|s|s.result.get("state").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)==Some("sent")).count();
            if out.violations.iter().any(|v|v.invariant_site==inv.site.to_string()&&v.viewer==*viewer&&v.reachability==Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace&&v.history.iter().filter(|s|s.result.get("state").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)==Some("sent")).count()==committed){continue;}
            match policy_in(bound,inv,rows,viewer,None,row,e){Ok(false)=>{},Ok(true)=>continue,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: {why}",inv.target_label()));continue;}}
            let Ok(key)=row.field("id")else{continue;};
            if inv.column.as_ref().and_then(|c|bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].written_by.get(c)).is_some_and(|gate|!gate.audience&&row.field(&gate.owner).ok()!=Some(&Value::Id(viewer.clone()))){continue 'actors;}

            // A typed authored row return is already a payload witness. It does
            // not require an invalid absent world that deletes referenced data.
            for bp in queries.iter().copied().filter(|p|typed_source(&p.result,&inv.table)){
                let cases=fixture_cases(&bp.program.args,rows,actors,out);
                if cases.len()>32{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: typed read-observer arguments capped at 32",bp.program.name));}
                for args in cases.into_iter().take(32){
                    if !reserve_observers(out,2){return;}
                    let args=match materialize_arguments(e,&bp.program.args,&args,viewer){Ok(a)=>a,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: typed history observer asset/input preparation: {why}",bp.program.name));continue;}};
                    out.attempted+=1;
                    if let Ok(q)=e.query(&bp.program.name,Context::new(viewer,args.clone()).at(NOW))&&q.complete&&!q.unavailable&&typed_disclosure(&bp.result,&q.data,&inv.table,key,inv.column.as_deref(),row){
                        out.attempted+=1;
                        if !matches!(e.query(&bp.program.name,Context::new(viewer,args.clone()).at(NOW)),Ok(again) if again.complete&&!again.unavailable&&again.data==q.data){
                            out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: typed read payload is not repeatable on the committed image",bp.program.name));continue;
                        }
                        violation(out,inv,steps,viewer,row,None,initial,if inv.column.is_some(){"column read observation"}else{"read"});
                        if let Some(v)=out.violations.last_mut(){v.operation=bp.program.name.clone();v.args=args;v.rule_path="authored query repeatably returned a compiler-typed row/column payload of the protected table while current policy is false".into();}
                        continue 'actors;
                    }
                }
            }
            if inv.column.as_deref().is_some_and(|c|c!="id"){continue 'actors;}
            if bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|t.columns.values().any(|k|matches!(k.required(),C::Ref{table} if table==&inv.table))){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: history paired absence needs a referentially valid closure",inv.table));continue;}
            let Ok(id)=key.as_str()else{continue;};
            let mut absent=match fork(bound,e,Vec::new()){Ok(e)=>e,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: absent world fork: {why}",inv.table));continue;}};
            if let Err(why)=absent.seed_delete_at(&inv.table,id,NOW){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: absent world refused: {why}",inv.table));continue;}
            let absent_rows=match readback(&absent){Ok(r)=>r,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: absent world readback: {why}",inv.table));continue;}};
            if policy_in(bound,inv,&absent_rows,viewer,None,row,&mut absent)!=Ok(false){continue;}
            for bp in queries.iter().copied().filter(|p|observing.iter().any(|t|super::super::audit::mentions(&serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap(),t))){
                let p=&bp.program;
                let cases=fixture_cases(&p.args,rows,actors,out);
                if cases.len()>32{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: read-observer arguments capped at 32; remaining combinations not covered",p.name));}
                for args in cases.into_iter().take(32){
                    if !reserve_observers(out,4){return;}
                    let raw_args=args;
                    let args=match materialize_arguments(e,&p.args,&raw_args,viewer){Ok(a)=>a,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: paired history observer asset/input preparation: {why}",p.name));continue;}};
                    if args!=raw_args{
                        absent=match fork(bound,e,Vec::new()){Ok(e)=>e,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: paired history argument ledger fork: {why}",p.name));continue;}};
                        if let Err(why)=absent.seed_delete_at(&inv.table,id,NOW){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: paired history argument ledger absence: {why}",p.name));continue;}
                        if readback(&absent)!=Ok(absent_rows.clone()){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: paired argument ledger changes the absent base world",p.name));continue;}
                    }
                    out.attempted+=2;
                    let (Ok(a),Ok(b))=(paired::observe(e,p,viewer,args.clone()),paired::observe(&mut absent,p,viewer,args.clone()))else{continue;};
                    if a==b{continue;}
                    if !matches!((paired::observe(e,p,viewer,args.clone()),paired::observe(&mut absent,p,viewer,args.clone())),(Ok(aa),Ok(bb)) if a==aa&&b==bb){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{} / {}: paired history observation is unstable or unavailable on repeat",p.name,inv.target_label()));continue;}
                    violation(out,inv,steps,viewer,row,None,initial,"read");
                    if let Some(v)=out.violations.last_mut(){v.operation=p.name.clone();v.args=args;v.observations=Some(paired::Observations{present:a,absent:b,absent_rows:absent_rows.clone()});}
                    continue 'actors;
                }
            }
        }}
    }
}
fn prior_policy(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,old:Option<&Value>,row:&Value,prior:&mut Option<Engine>)->Result<bool,String>{
    match prior.as_mut(){Some(e)=>policy_in(bound,inv,rows,viewer,old,row,e),None=>policy(bound,inv,rows,viewer,old,row)}
}
fn assertions(bound:&Bound,before:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,after:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,steps:&[Step],prior:&mut Option<Engine>,current:&mut Engine,out:&mut Campaign){
    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="always"){
        for row in after.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){
            let old=before.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok());
            if policy_in(bound,inv,after,viewer,old,row,current)!=Ok(false){continue;}
            // A pre-existing seed defect is supplemental; a newly violated
            // assertion or changed image is an authored committed observation.
            if old!=Some(row)||old.is_some_and(|r|prior_policy(bound,inv,before,viewer,Some(r),r,prior)==Ok(true)){
                violation(out,inv,steps,viewer,row,old,initial,"always");
            }
        }
    }
}
fn changed(bound:&Bound,p:&Program,before:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,after:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,steps:&[Step],prior:&mut Option<Engine>,current:&mut Engine,out:&mut Campaign){
    assertions(bound,before,after,initial,viewer,steps,prior,current,out);
    if !super::super::census::body(&p.body){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: loop/opaque write boundary requires executed-effect tracing",p.name));return;}
    for inv in &bound.invariants{
        if matches!(inv.target.as_str(),"read"|"always")||inv.operation.as_ref().is_some_and(|op|op!=&p.name){continue;}
        let judged;
        let inv=if inv.target=="always"{inv}else{
            let Some(stable)=stability::necessary(bound,p,inv)else{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{} / {}: unknown/implicit policy changes need executed-effect tracing",p.name,inv.target_label()));continue;};
            judged=stable;&judged
        };
        // A net image cannot identify an intermediate write or implicit effect.
        let writes=ir::write_tables(p).into_iter().filter(|(t,_)|t==&inv.table).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        if writes.len()!=1 {out.unsupported.insert(format!("{} / {}.{}: multiple or implicit writes require executed-effect tracing",p.name,inv.table,inv.target));continue;}
        if (inv.target=="delete"||inv.operation.is_some())&&writes[0].1!="delete"&&before.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().any(|old|!after.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().any(|r|r.field("id").ok()==old.field("id").ok())){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: commit-end/implicit retirement is not an executed delete",p.name));}
        if (inv.target=="delete"||inv.operation.is_some())&&writes[0].1=="delete"{
            for old in before.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().filter(|old|!bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].expired(old,NOW)&&!after.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().any(|r|r.field("id").ok()==old.field("id").ok())){
                let deps=inv.policy.as_ref().map(kernel::dependencies).unwrap_or_default();
                if ir::write_tables(p).iter().any(|(t,_)|deps.contains(t)&&t!=&inv.table){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: delete policy dependency changes before disclosure",p.name));continue;}
                if prior_policy(bound,inv,before,viewer,Some(old),old,prior)==Ok(false){violation(out,inv,steps,viewer,old,Some(old),initial,"delete");}
            }
        }
        for row in after.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){
            let old=before.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok()&&!bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].expired(r,NOW));
            if old==Some(row){continue;}
            let effect=if old.is_some(){"update"}else{"insert"};
            if !super::super::effects(inv).contains(&effect){continue;}
            if inv.column.as_ref().is_some_and(|c|old.is_some_and(|o|o.field(c).ok()==row.field(c).ok())){continue;}
            if inv.target=="always" {
                if policy_in(bound,inv,after,viewer,old,row,current)==Ok(false){violation(out,inv,steps,viewer,row,old,initial,effect);}
            }else{
                let deps=inv.policy.as_ref().map(kernel::dependencies).unwrap_or_default();
                if ir::write_tables(p).iter().any(|(t,_)|deps.contains(t)&&t!=&inv.table) {out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: write policy dependency changes within the operation; tracing required",p.name));continue;}
                if prior_policy(bound,inv,before,viewer,old,row,prior)==Ok(false)||old.is_some_and(|old|prior_policy(bound,inv,before,viewer,Some(old),old,prior)==Ok(false)){
                    violation(out,inv,steps,viewer,row,old,initial,effect);
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
fn role_candidates<'a>(bound:&Bound,p:&'a Program,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,actors:&[String],cases:&[BTreeMap<String,Value>],fixture:&Engine)->Vec<(&'a Program,String,BTreeMap<String,Value>)>{
    let mut out=Vec::new();let mut groups=Vec::new();
    let json=serde_json::to_string(p).unwrap();
    let Ok(mut judge)=fork(bound,fixture,Vec::new())else{return out;};
    for (table,_) in ir::write_tables(p){
        let columns=super::super::privilege::role_columns(bound,&table);
        if !columns.iter().any(|c|json.contains(&format!("\"{c}\":"))){continue;}
        let t=&bound.schema.tables[&table];
        for row in initial.get(&table).into_iter().flatten(){
            let principal=t.columns.iter().find_map(|(c,k)|matches!(k.required(),C::Principal).then(||row.field(c).ok()).flatten());
            let Some(principal)=principal else{continue;};
            let mut targets=Vec::new();
            for case in cases{
                let mut args=case.clone();
                for (name,kind) in &p.args{match kind{
                    ir::ArgType::Principal=>{args.insert(name.clone(),principal.clone());},
                    ir::ArgType::Ref{table:target} if target==&table=>{if let Ok(id)=row.field("id"){args.insert(name.clone(),id.clone());}},_=>{}
                }}
                if !targets.contains(&args){targets.push(args);}
            }
            let mut row_cases=Vec::new();
            for actor in actors{
                let owner=columns.iter().filter_map(|c|t.written_by.get(c)).any(|a|row.field(&a.owner).ok()==Some(&Value::Id(actor.clone())));
                let gate=judge.fixture_policy(&t.update_rule_judged(),actor,Some(row),row,NOW).ok();
                if !owner&&gate!=Some(rules::Verdict::Readable){continue;}
                for args in &targets{row_cases.push((p,actor.clone(),args.clone()));}
            }
            groups.push(row_cases);
        }
    }
    for n in 0..groups.iter().map(Vec::len).max().unwrap_or(0){for g in &groups{if let Some(case)=g.get(n){out.push(case.clone());}}}
    out
}
pub(super) fn run(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,out:&mut Campaign){
    if bound.invariants.is_empty(){return;}
    let initial=match readback(fixture){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);return;}};
    if initial.values().map(Vec::len).sum::<usize>()>1000{out.unsupported.insert("history fixture exceeds 1000 rows".into());return;}
    let actors=actors(bound,&initial);
    let probes=Vec::new();
    let mut groups=Vec::new();
    for bp in bound.programs.iter().filter(|p|p.program.kind==ProgramKind::Mutation&&!p.program.native){
        let cases=fixture_cases(&bp.program.args,&initial,&actors,out);
        let mut group=role_candidates(bound,&bp.program,&initial,&actors,&cases,fixture);
        // Diagonal order gives both actors and argument values an early turn.
        for shift in 0..actors.len(){for n in 0..cases.len().max(actors.len()){
            if cases.is_empty(){break;}
            group.push((&bp.program,actors[(n+shift)%actors.len()].clone(),cases[n%cases.len()].clone()));
        }}
        if !group.is_empty(){groups.push(group);}
    }
    let mut candidates=Vec::new();
    for n in 0..groups.iter().map(Vec::len).max().unwrap_or(0){for g in &groups{if let Some(c)=g.get(n){candidates.push(c.clone());}}}
    let mut base=match fork(bound,fixture,probes.clone()){Ok(e)=>e,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);return;}};
    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="always"){
        for row in initial.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){for viewer in &actors{
            if policy_in(bound,inv,&initial,viewer,None,row,&mut base)!=Ok(false){continue;}
            let mut witness=Campaign::default();violation(&mut witness,inv,&[],viewer,row,None,&initial,"always");
            if let Some(mut v)=witness.violations.pop(){v.reachability=Reachability::TrustedSeedOnly;v.operation="$fixture.assertion".into();v.rule_path="initial trusted fixture assertion; no authored mutation".into();v.scope="definite violation in trusted seed; no authored transition claimed; non-failing supplemental evidence".into();out.violations.push(v);}break;
        }}
    }
    observe(bound,&mut base,&initial,&initial,&actors,&[],out);
    // Spend half the pair budget on grant changes followed by an operation
    // as the affected principal; interleave first steps and successor names.
    let mut role_groups=Vec::new();
    for (i,(p,_,args)) in candidates.iter().enumerate().take(LIMIT){
        let json=serde_json::to_string(p).unwrap();
        let rosters=ir::write_tables(p).into_iter().filter_map(|(t,_)|{
            let roles=super::super::privilege::role_columns(bound,&t);
            roles.iter().any(|c|json.contains(&format!("\"{c}\":"))).then_some(t)
        }).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
        if rosters.is_empty(){continue;}
        let mut recipients=BTreeSet::new();
        for (name,kind) in &p.args {if let Some(value)=args.get(name){match kind {
            ir::ArgType::Principal=>{if let Value::Id(actor)=value{recipients.insert(actor.clone());}},
            ir::ArgType::Ref{table} if rosters.contains(table)=>{
                if let Some(row)=initial.get(table).into_iter().flatten().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==Some(value)){
                    for (column,kind) in &bound.schema.tables[table].columns{if matches!(kind.required(),C::Principal)&&let Ok(Value::Id(actor))=row.field(column){recipients.insert(actor.clone());}}
                }
            },_=>{}
        }}}
        let mut seen=BTreeSet::new();
        let successors=candidates.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(j,(next,actor,_))|(next.name!=p.name&&recipients.contains(actor)&&seen.insert((next.name.clone(),actor.clone()))).then_some((i,j))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        if !successors.is_empty(){role_groups.push(successors);}
    }
    let mut role_pairs=Vec::new();
    'pairs:for n in 0..role_groups.iter().map(Vec::len).max().unwrap_or(0){for group in &role_groups{if let Some(pair)=group.get(n){role_pairs.push(*pair);if role_pairs.len()==LIMIT/2{break 'pairs;}}}}
    // Breadth first, then targeted and diagonal pairs: no first step owns the cap.
    let singles=candidates.len().min(LIMIT);
    let mut setups=Vec::new();let mut role_changes=Vec::new();
    let mut setup_seen=BTreeSet::new();let mut role_seen=BTreeSet::new();let mut pair_plan=Vec::new();
    for n in 0..singles+LIMIT {
        if n==singles&&!candidates.is_empty(){
            let mut seen=BTreeSet::new();
            'setup:for &second in &role_changes{for &first in &setups{if seen.insert((first,second)){pair_plan.push((first,second));if pair_plan.len()==LIMIT/2{break 'setup;}}}}
            for &(first,second) in &role_pairs{if pair_plan.len()==LIMIT{break;}if seen.insert((first,second)){pair_plan.push((first,second));}}
            let mut k=0;
            while pair_plan.len()<LIMIT{pair_plan.push((k%candidates.len(),(k/candidates.len()+k+1)%candidates.len()));k+=1;}
        }
        let indices=if n<singles{vec![n]}else if candidates.is_empty(){break;}else{let (first,second)=pair_plan[n-singles];vec![first,second]};
        let mut engine=match fork(bound,fixture,probes.clone()){Ok(e)=>e,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);break;}};
        out.sequences+=1;let mut steps=Vec::new();
        for i in indices{
            let (p,viewer,args)=&candidates[i];let before=match readback(&engine){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);break;}};
            let args=match materialize_arguments(&mut engine,&p.args,args,viewer){Ok(a)=>a,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: history asset arguments: {why}",p.name));break;}};
            let mut prior=if bound.invariants.iter().any(|i|i.target!="read"&&i.policy.as_ref().is_some_and(RuleExpr::has_reference)){match fork(bound,&engine,Vec::new()){Ok(e)=>Some(e),Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(why);None}}}else{None};
            out.attempted+=1;*out.operations.entry(p.name.clone()).or_default()+=1;
            match engine.mutate(&p.name,None,Context::new(viewer,args.clone()).at(NOW)){
                Ok(result)=>{
                    let sent=matches!(result.state,snapback4_core::WriteState::Sent{..});
                    steps.push(Step{operation:p.name.clone(),viewer:viewer.clone(),args:args.clone(),result:serde_json::to_value(&result.state).unwrap()});
                    let after=match readback(&engine){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);break;}};
                    if sent&&n<singles{
                        let signature=serde_json::to_string(&(&p.name,viewer,&candidates[i].2)).unwrap();
                        let new_media=bound.invariants.iter().filter(|inv|inv.target=="read"&&initial.get(&inv.table).is_none_or(Vec::is_empty)).any(|inv|{
                            let table=&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table];
                            after.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().any(|row|table.columns.iter().any(|(column,kind)|kind.contains_asset()&&row.field(column).is_ok_and(|v|*v!=Value::Null&&!matches!(v,Value::Array(xs) if xs.is_empty()))))
                        });
                        if new_media&&setup_seen.insert(signature.clone()){setups.push(i);}
                        let changed_role=bound.schema.tables.iter().any(|(table,_)|{
                            let columns=super::super::privilege::role_columns(bound,table);
                            !columns.is_empty()&&before.get(table).into_iter().flatten().any(|old|after.get(table).into_iter().flatten().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==old.field("id").ok()).is_some_and(|new|columns.iter().any(|c|old.field(c).ok()!=new.field(c).ok())))
                        });
                        if changed_role&&role_seen.insert(signature){role_changes.push(i);}
                    }
                    if sent{changed(bound,p,&before,&after,&initial,viewer,&steps,&mut prior,&mut engine,out);}else{out.refused+=1;}
                    if sent&&before!=after{observe(bound,&mut engine,&after,&initial,&actors,&steps,out);column_reads::after_history(bound,&engine,&initial,&steps,out);}
                },Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: history engine error {e}",p.name));break;}
            }
        }
    }
    out.unsupported.insert("history breadth/pairs bounded to 512; unvisited and longer traces remain uncovered".into());
}
//! CLI security reporting; compile/test evidence share one analyzer.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#5-exposure-report — full audit is opt-in, intent notes are on ordinary check.
use super::*;
use snapback4_lang::security::{self,Report};
mod http;
pub(super) fn unauthenticated(bound:&Bound,sources:&[(String,String)])->bool {
    match http::run(bound,sources){Ok(_)=>true,Err(why)=>{eprintln!("security HTTP failure: {why}");false}}
}
pub(super) fn report(project:&Project,bound:&Bound,options:&Options)->Report{
    let mut report=security::analyze(bound);
    if let Ok(sources)=project.sources(){security::add_source_notes(&mut report,&sources,bound);}
    let mut campaign=security::challenges::Campaign::default();
    if !bound.invariants.is_empty(){
        let mut selection=options.clone();
        if !selection.flags.contains_key("fixture")&&project.root.join("fixtures").is_dir(){selection.flags.insert("fixture".into(),project.root.join("fixtures").to_string_lossy().into_owned());}
        let fixture=if selection.flags.contains_key("fixture"){
            super::test_fixture::engine_at(project,bound,&selection,1_800_000_000_000)
        }else{
            campaign.unsupported.insert("no supplied fixture: histories start at the validated empty store; populated production states remain uncovered".into());
            snapback4_core::Store::memory().and_then(|store|snapback4_core::Engine::new_at(store,bound.schema.clone(),bound.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone()),1_800_000_000_000)).map_err(|why|why.to_string())
        };
        match fixture{
            Ok(fixture)=>security::challenges::fixture_observers(bound,&fixture,&mut campaign),
            Err(why)=>for verdict in &mut report.invariants{verdict.reasons.push(format!("fixture reachability not covered: {why}"));},
        }
    }
    let sites=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Unproved&&!campaign.violations.iter().any(|c|c.invariant_site==v.invariant.site.to_string()&&c.reachability==security::challenges::Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace)).map(|v|v.invariant.site.to_string()).collect();
    let campaign=security::challenges::run_selected_after(bound,Some(&sites),campaign);
    security::challenges::apply(&mut report,&campaign);report
}
pub fn audit(options:&Options)->i32{
    let project=project_or_exit();
    let bound=compiled_or_exit(match project.compile(){Ok(b)=>b,Err(e)=>{eprintln!("audit: {e}");return 2;}},options.json);
    let report=report(&project,&bound,options);
    if options.json {println!("{}",serde_json::to_string(&report).unwrap());}
    else {print!("{}",report.text_with_all(options.flags.contains_key("all")));}
    if report.refuted(){1}else{0}
}
pub(super) fn summary(report:&Report,all:bool){
    for v in &report.invariants {if !all&&v.status==security::Status::Refuted&&v.counterexample.as_ref().is_some_and(|c|c.reachability!=security::challenges::Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace){eprintln!("security: {} refuted by seed/synthetic witness only (non-failing; --all for detail)",v.invariant.target_label());continue;}eprintln!("security {:?}: {} {}",v.status,v.invariant.target_label(),v.invariant.predicate);for why in &v.reasons{eprintln!("  {why}");}if let Some(c)=&v.counterexample{eprintln!("  counterexample {}",serde_json::to_string(c).unwrap());}}
    for note in &report.notes{eprintln!("{} {}: {} — {}",note.site,note.code,note.message,note.rewrite);}
    if !report.invariants.is_empty(){eprintln!("security: audit --json lists paths, exclusions and grant writers; guide security explains proof scope");}
}
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#4-counterexamples-and-generated-tests — qualify the installed policy surface and its finite campaigns.
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { createHash } from "node:crypto";
import { copyFileSync, mkdirSync, mkdtempSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { join, resolve } from "node:path";
import { spawnSync } from "node:child_process";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { runToolchainCommand } from "./publication-starters.mjs";

const sha256 = path => createHash("sha256").update(readFileSync(path)).digest("hex");

/** Execute only the supplied packed client/CLI; never build or repack source.
 * Evidence contains counters and verdicts, never credentials or HTTP bodies. */
export async function proveSecurity(clientTarball, platformTarball, directory) {
  const client = resolve(clientTarball), platform = resolve(platformTarball);
  const artifacts = { clientSha256: sha256(client), platformSha256: sha256(platform) };
  const parent = resolve(directory); mkdirSync(parent, { recursive: true });
  const cwd = mkdtempSync(join(parent, "security-policy-"));
  const env = { ...process.env, CI: "1", npm_config_audit: "false", npm_config_fund: "false" };
  delete env.SNAPBACK4_BIN; delete env.PORT_LO; delete env.PORT_HI;
  writeFileSync(join(cwd, "package.json"), '{"private":true,"type":"module"}');
  await runToolchainCommand("npm", ["install", "--ignore-scripts", "--omit=optional", "--no-audit", "--no-fund", client, platform], {
    cwd, env, timeout: 300_000,
  });
  const cli = join(cwd, "node_modules/snapback4/bin/snapback4.js");
  const native = join(cwd, "node_modules", `snapback4-${process.platform}-${process.arch}`, "snapback4");
  const binarySha256 = sha256(native), commands = [];
  function command(args, project = cwd, expected = 0) {
    const result = spawnSync(process.execPath, [cli, ...args], {
      cwd: project, env, encoding: "utf8", timeout: 120_000, maxBuffer: 32 * 1024 * 1024,
    });
    commands.push({ args, project: project.slice(cwd.length) || ".", exitCode: result.status, expectedExitCode: expected });
    // Child output can contain replayed rows. Keep failure diagnostics to stable
    // codes and process state rather than serializing potentially private rows.
    const codes = [...new Set(((result.stdout ?? "") + (result.stderr ?? "")).match(/\b[EN]_[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]+\b/g) ?? [])];
    assert.equal(result.error, undefined, `${args.join(" ")}: ${result.error?.code ?? "process error"}`);
    assert.equal(result.status, expected, `${args.join(" ")}: expected exit ${expected}, got ${result.status}; codes: ${codes.join(", ")}`);
    return result.stdout;
  }
  function schemaProject(name, source) {
    const project = join(cwd, name); mkdirSync(join(project, "snapback"), { recursive: true });
    writeFileSync(join(project, "snapback/schema.q"), source); return project;
  }
  function checked(project) {
    const result = JSON.parse(command(["check", "--json"], project));
    assert.equal(result.ok, true); assert.ok(result.security?.invariants.length > 0);
    assert.ok(result.security.invariants.every(verdict => ["proved", "unproved"].includes(verdict.status)));
    return result.security;
  }
  function campaign(project, requireHttp = false) {
    const output = command(["test", "--fixture-only"], project);
    const counts = /security tests: (\d+) attempted, (\d+) refused, (\d+) reachable violations; (\d+) seed\/synthetic observations \(non-failing\); finite campaign, not proof/.exec(output);
    assert.ok(counts, "default test executes the generated finite security campaign");
    assert.ok(Number(counts[1]) > 0, "the security campaign actually attempts challenges");
    assert.equal(Number(counts[3]), 0);
    assert.doesNotMatch(output, /security boundary failure:|security HTTP failure:|security violation:/);
    const http = /security unauthenticated serve HTTP: (\d+) attempts refused/.exec(output);
    const artifactBoundary = "security not covered: unauthenticated serve HTTP for native/action/effect applications needs its complete deployed artifacts";
    if (requireHttp) assert.ok(http && Number(http[1]) > 0, "the isolated production HTTP campaign runs");
    else assert.ok((http && Number(http[1]) > 0) || output.includes(artifactBoundary), "HTTP coverage is executed or explicitly bounded by deployed artifacts");
    return { attempted: Number(counts[1]), refused: Number(counts[2]), violations: Number(counts[3]), seedSyntheticObservations: Number(counts[4]), unauthenticatedHttpRefused: Number(http?.[1] ?? 0),
      ...(http ? {} : { unauthenticatedHttpNotCovered: "complete deployed native/action/effect artifacts required" }),
      revocationHttpRefused: Number(/security live-session revocation HTTP: (\d+) attempts refused/.exec(output)?.[1] ?? 0) };
  }

  const guide = command(["guide", "security", "--full"]);
  const blocks = [...guide.matchAll(/```quarry\n([\s\S]*?)\n```/g)].map(match => match[1]);
  assert.ok(blocks.length > 0, "installed security guide contains executable Quarry");
  const guideCases = [];
  for (const [index, source] of blocks.entries()) {
    const project = schemaProject(`card-${index}`, source), security = checked(project);
    assert.ok(security.invariants.every(verdict => verdict.direct_rule === "proved"));
    assert.match(readFileSync(join(project, "snapback/generated/security-invariants.q.draft"), "utf8"), /DRAFT: NOT ACCEPTED/);
    const audited = JSON.parse(command(["audit", "--json"], project));
    assert.equal(audited.format, 1); assert.ok(audited.exposures.some(exposure => exposure.table === "privateNotes"));
    assert.ok(audited.exposures.some(exposure => exposure.table === "privateNotes" && exposure.column_audiences?.body), "installed audit exposes the body column audience");
    const checkAudit = JSON.parse(command(["check", "--audit", "--json"], project));
    assert.equal(checkAudit.format, 1); assert.deepEqual(checkAudit, audited, "check --audit is the structured audit alias");
    const finite = campaign(project, true);
    const broad = source.replace("read <- .patient = viewer", "read <- allow");
    assert.notEqual(broad, source, "the installed card's private read is widened for the counterexample");
    writeFileSync(join(project, "snapback/schema.q"), broad);
    const refuted = JSON.parse(command(["check", "--json"], project, 1));
    assert.equal(refuted.ok, false);
    const readVerdicts = refuted.security.invariants.filter(verdict => verdict.invariant.target === "read");
    assert.equal(readVerdicts.length, 2, "the installed card exercises row and column read intent");
    assert.ok(readVerdicts.every(verdict => verdict.status === "refuted" && verdict.counterexample?.reachability === "authored-fixture-trace"), "ordinary writes from the empty initial store lead to actual row/column disclosure");
    const auditRefuted = JSON.parse(command(["audit", "--json"], project, 1));
    assert.ok(auditRefuted.invariants.some(verdict => verdict.status === "refuted"));
    command(["test", "--fixture-only"], project, 1);
    guideCases.push({ block: index, invariants: security.invariants.length, audit: "passed", finite, broadRead: "refuted", expectedFailureExits: [1, 1, 1] });
  }

  const sessions = [];
  for (const method of ["guests", "password"]) {
    const project = schemaProject(`revocation-${method}`, `use identity ${method}
table notes:
  owner: principal
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  online only
invariant notes.read only .owner = viewer
query notes():
  return notes first 10 by .id
mutation add():
  return insert notes { owner: viewer }
`);
    checked(project);
    const finite = campaign(project, true);
    assert.equal(finite.revocationHttpRefused, 14, "actual refresh and logout-all retire both credentials at all seven doors");
    sessions.push({ method, status: "passed", ...finite, disconnectedDevice: "not covered" });
  }

  const starters = [];
  for (const starter of [
    { id: "todo-online", flags: ["--online"], intent: "invariant todos.read only .ownerId = viewer" },
    { id: "social", flags: ["--template", "social"] },
    { id: "todo-web", flags: ["--web"] },
    { id: "chat-web", flags: ["--template", "chat", "--web"], intent: "invariant messages.read only exists memberships[.conversationId, viewer]" },
    { id: "chat-expo", flags: ["--template", "chat", "--expo"], intent: "invariant messages.read only exists memberships[.conversationId, viewer]" },
  ]) {
    const project = join(cwd, starter.id); command(["init", project, ...starter.flags]);
    const source = readFileSync(join(project, "snapback/schema.q"), "utf8");
    if (starter.intent) assert.ok(source.includes(starter.intent), `${starter.id} ships its independent intent`);
    const security = checked(project), finite = campaign(project);
    starters.push({ id: starter.id, status: "passed", invariants: security.invariants.length, ...finite });
  }
  const production = schemaProject("production-recipe", "");
  copyFileSync(join(cwd, "node_modules/snapback4/dist/recipes/production/web/schema.q"), join(production, "snapback/schema.q"));
  const productionSecurity = checked(production);
  starters.push({ id: "production-recipe", status: "passed", source: "installed dist/recipes/production/web/schema.q", invariants: productionSecurity.invariants.length, ...campaign(production) });
  assert.equal(starters.length, 6);

  assert.equal(sha256(native), binarySha256, "installed CLI binary is unchanged");
  assert.equal(sha256(client), artifacts.clientSha256, "supplied client tarball is unchanged");
  assert.equal(sha256(platform), artifacts.platformSha256, "supplied platform tarball is unchanged");
  const result = { status: "passed", guide: "verbatim installed security card", artifacts: { ...artifacts, binarySha256 }, guideCases, sessions, starters,
    scope: "finite campaigns and declared policy checks; no zero-hole or disconnected-device revocation claim", commands };
  writeFileSync(join(cwd, "security-evidence.json"), JSON.stringify(result, null, 2) + "\n");
  return result;
}

if (process.argv[1] && resolve(process.argv[1]) === fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)) {
  const [client, platform, directory] = process.argv.slice(2);
  if (!client || !platform || !directory) throw Error("usage: publication-security.mjs client.tgz platform.tgz output-directory");
  console.log(JSON.stringify(await proveSecurity(client, platform, directory)));
}

## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

/bin/bash -lc "timeout 10s sed -n '895,960p' llp/3000.036.004-security-invariants-and-exposure.rfc.md" in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-sec3
 succeeded in 0ms:
advice requires a principal/role unique roster; per-resource context keys need
a separate delegation reduction. Delegation guards constrain the actual role
column of that roster, never an ordinary project/status lookup. Assignment
intent is selected by effect, with upsert arms judged separately. Initial row
assertion failures remain visible as TrustedSeedOnly, even without mutations.
The operation matrix uses a shared remaining-call counter and fair per-operation
quotas of 1–512; above 8192 eligible operations, unvisited names are explicit.

For fixed ordinary enrollment, row-scoped permissions alone do not warrant
escalation advice. Without contrary independent intent, a fixed assignment
requires new unconditional read/delete or roster authority to emit the note;
caller-chosen roles retain the broader effect comparison. This advisory is not
an all-operation reachability theorem; scoped-resource delegation and fixed
write-only privilege distinctions require independent intent/reductions.
The report keeps the shortest reachable witness. Campaigns can also retain
separate one- and two-step examples without replacing a shorter trace or
attributing a pre-existing disclosure to an unrelated mutation. Named-operation
intent covers all its target-table effects; table/column insert/update/delete
intent covers only that executed arm. Column prefilters and paired intent use
validated committed images; unsupported synthetic operation judgments are named.

History read observers first select compiler-typed row-return candidates or the
transitive maintained/through/raw-probe table closure for scalar observations.
They reserve at most 32768 authored read calls per campaign, including repeats;
remaining disclosures are explicit uncovered work. Mutation sequence replay
continues within its own bounds. Read-call exhaustion never grants a proof.

Tuple-key role tests use the same column-aware normalization in lifetime advice.
A scoped enum context is not a role qualifier; qualifying unique keys contain
one principal and actual role qualifiers only. Named-operation delegation intent
is honored for that caller operation. Initial fixture column observations run
before history prefixes and synthetic exploration; continuation shares the same
work counters so a later phase cannot evict a zero-step witness by consuming its
budget first. §12.8's independent-current-role requirement supersedes the older
name-based alternative in §12.6.

Fixed-role comparison retains any additional probe context as an opaque
predicate; a matching role alone cannot make a region-qualified read globally
unconditional. Lifetime advice excludes a retained branch that already implies
the declared policy, including explicit owner alternatives. Arbitrarily named
role qualifiers require unambiguous literal-probe evidence before a compound
principal/qualifier key can be treated as a global roster.

Synthetic enumeration deduplicates identical candidate seed rows and typed
operation arguments after dependency pruning and literal pinning. Unrelated
schema enum digits cannot cause repeated replay of the same input. This is
finite input exploration, with no exhaustive entropy/generated-id claim.

A different or negated current-role check does not discharge retained-grant
intent: only implication of the applicable independent policy suppresses that
advice. Live roster probes are not themselves described as retained grants.
Repeated risky branches within one table/effect produce one located advisory;
audit retains their individual audiences and writers.

An initial column pass visits every sampled actor within its shared budget,
retaining each actor's first disclosure instead of stopping after the first
viewer. Later unrelated prefixes cannot replace an already observed zero-step
exposure for another sampled viewer.

Unprojected compiler-typed Row/Page payloads also witness column reads when the
returned field equals that committed physical value and its field-visibility
gate admits this viewer. This covers real media ids without manufacturing an
alternate asset ledger. Projected scalar/shape values retain the paired-world
requirement; a coincidentally matching constant is not column provenance. A
private field's redacted null is never treated as disclosure of a null value.

 succeeded in 0ms:

Absent contrary independent intent, caller-chosen role advice requires new
unconditional table authority; an additional trusted resource/ownership grant
is a scoped delegation boundary requiring its own reduction. Fixed enrollment
keeps the narrower read/delete/roster comparison. This avoids treating ordinary
self-enrollment for one's own records as privileged provisioning.

Principal-only global membership grants also participate in advice. An
unqualified viewer probe of a required unique principal denotes current
membership; authored deletion/principal reassignment can revoke it. Membership
creation receives escalation advice when it contradicts a pure caller-based
independent provisioning invariant (table insert/update/write or named operation).
Principal reassignment uses update authority including private-field ownership;
upsert creation and reassignment are judged on their separate arms. A dominating
global appointer role may reside in a separate roster, with all its qualifiers
retained when checking implication.
Ordinary account enrollment without such provisioning intent is not guessed to
be an administrator grant. Scoped/qualified membership still needs its reduction.

Paired query observations tag successful data separately from authored logical
refusals, retaining refusal codes and messages. Only engine-provided authored
rule/constraint markers qualify; incomplete reads, resource exhaustion and
ambiguous engine errors remain outside logical comparison. This prevents both
missed refusal oracles and collisions between a payload shape and a refusal.
Synthetic principal sampling separates the focal row's attribution from the
supporting roster/assignment recipient; finite enumeration still never proves
that all tuples are covered.

Within the unchanged 512-pair cap, up to 256 pairs prioritize a global-role
mutation followed by a distinct operation as the named affected principal.
Ref arguments resolve through fixture roster rows; scalar principal arguments
supply other recipients. First steps and successor operation names interleave;
remaining slots retain diagonal pairs. These are candidates only: real commits,
authorized policies and stable necessary conditions still decide witnesses.

The 16384 column budget counts call/world reservations: each world variant,
authority-judgment actor, visited observer program and generated snapshot reserves
a slot even when it declines before execution; each paired experiment reserves
four calls for primary, alternative and repeats. Typed history payload witnesses
reserve two read calls and require identical complete data on the committed image.
A failed repeat is named unsupported, never a reachable failing counterexample.

Write-arm predecessor matching uses physical unexpired rows, including rows
whose `not before` time is pending; read visibility never converts an update
into insert or hides an executed delete. Every generated and fixture column pair
compares both committed readbacks for non-protected base fields/captures and
populations, and records the actual committed alternate world. Repeatability
also checks those input worlds, not just the returned logical observations.

Whole-row paired observers use typed fixture argument products (32 per observer),
including both synthetic principals independently of the row's owner; state and
argument variants must not accidentally force every aggregate lookup to the
opposite owner. A shared 8192-call reservation budget covers primary/alternate
and repeats; exhaustion explicitly names the remaining uncovered worlds.

Column comparison also requires identical materialized observer arguments across
both worlds and repeats. Independently generated asset-input identities are an
explicit coupled-ledger blocker, not evidence about the protected column.

Time arguments sample one day before the campaign clock, the clock itself and
one day after; this controls input values, not STAGE/MIG's host clock. Singles
also retain successful unique candidates that populate an initially empty
read-protected media table and those that change an existing global role.
Up to 256 pair slots replay media creation then a role change, including changes
by another caller; actual uploaded media and both commits remain in the trace.
Remaining pair slots prefer the role-successor plan above, then diagonal pairs.
The 512-single/512-pair limits do not increase. This exercises stale media grants
without inventing asset-bearing seed rows or silently claiming longer histories.

History read observers materialize actor-owned image/file inputs before querying,
reuse those exact inputs for repeats, and clone the verified ledger into paired
absence worlds. Upload preparation failures and every failed typed/paired repeat
name their operation/invariant coverage blocker; they never silently clear an
obligation. Column pairs still decline independently materialized asset inputs
when their identities differ, as required by their fixed-input comparison.

Authority advice conjoins all supplied owned-field old-owner gates; it does not
OR ownership with the table gate. Only a statically exclusive owned-field patch
may bypass an unnamed update rule. Mixed patches and named transitions retain
the judged table gate, including private fields it needs to inspect. Insert
ownership applies to supplied fields, not omitted defaults. Named next-image
role literals participate in target-role substitution. The same calculation
governs role assignments and principal-only membership reassignment.

An update gate that implies the role/principal is unchanged is not an escalation
witness, even when its source patch explicitly supplies that field. Runtime
regressions cover owned-only, mixed, named-deny, named-no-change and named-target
restrictions for role writes, plus owned principal-only membership reassignment.

A validated empty fixture/store is also a trusted initial state. Histories do
not switch off merely because no authored rows exist: ordinary operations may
create data and subsequent authored queries may disclose it. Such traces retain
their real mutation history and authored-fixture origin. A query-only empty app
still cannot turn arbitrary seeded/synthetic rows into reachable failures. The
packed security-card proof executes its writers from empty state and requires
row and column disclosures to carry that reachable origin when the rule widens.

//! CLI security reporting; compile/test evidence share one analyzer.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#5-exposure-report — full audit is opt-in, intent notes are on ordinary check.
use super::*;
use snapback4_lang::security::{self,Report};
mod http;
pub(super) fn unauthenticated(bound:&Bound,sources:&[(String,String)])->bool {
    match http::run(bound,sources){Ok(_)=>true,Err(why)=>{eprintln!("security HTTP failure: {why}");false}}
}
pub(super) fn report(project:&Project,bound:&Bound,options:&Options)->Report{
    let mut report=security::analyze(bound);
    if let Ok(sources)=project.sources(){security::add_source_notes(&mut report,&sources,bound);}
    let mut campaign=security::challenges::Campaign::default();
    if !bound.invariants.is_empty(){
        let mut selection=options.clone();
        if !selection.flags.contains_key("fixture")&&project.root.join("fixtures").is_dir(){selection.flags.insert("fixture".into(),project.root.join("fixtures").to_string_lossy().into_owned());}
        let fixture=if selection.flags.contains_key("fixture"){
            super::test_fixture::engine_at(project,bound,&selection,1_800_000_000_000)
        }else{
            campaign.unsupported.insert("no supplied fixture: histories start at the validated empty store; populated production states remain uncovered".into());
            snapback4_core::Store::memory().and_then(|store|snapback4_core::Engine::new_at(store,bound.schema.clone(),bound.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone()),1_800_000_000_000)).map_err(|why|why.to_string())
        };
        match fixture{
            Ok(fixture)=>security::challenges::fixture_observers(bound,&fixture,&mut campaign),
            Err(why)=>for verdict in &mut report.invariants{verdict.reasons.push(format!("fixture reachability not covered: {why}"));},
        }
    }
    let sites=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Unproved&&!campaign.violations.iter().any(|c|c.invariant_site==v.invariant.site.to_string()&&c.reachability==security::challenges::Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace)).map(|v|v.invariant.site.to_string()).collect();
    let campaign=security::challenges::run_selected_after(bound,Some(&sites),campaign);
    security::challenges::apply(&mut report,&campaign);report
}
pub fn audit(options:&Options)->i32{
    let project=project_or_exit();
    let bound=compiled_or_exit(match project.compile(){Ok(b)=>b,Err(e)=>{eprintln!("audit: {e}");return 2;}},options.json);
    let report=report(&project,&bound,options);
    if options.json {println!("{}",serde_json::to_string(&report).unwrap());}
    else {print!("{}",report.text_with_all(options.flags.contains_key("all")));}
    if report.refuted(){1}else{0}
}
pub(super) fn summary(report:&Report,all:bool){
    for v in &report.invariants {if !all&&v.status==security::Status::Refuted&&v.counterexample.as_ref().is_some_and(|c|c.reachability!=security::challenges::Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace){eprintln!("security: {} refuted by seed/synthetic witness only (non-failing; --all for detail)",v.invariant.target_label());continue;}eprintln!("security {:?}: {} {}",v.status,v.invariant.target_label(),v.invariant.predicate);for why in &v.reasons{eprintln!("  {why}");}if let Some(c)=&v.counterexample{eprintln!("  counterexample {}",serde_json::to_string(c).unwrap());}}
    for note in &report.notes{eprintln!("{} {}: {} — {}",note.site,note.code,note.message,note.rewrite);}
    if !report.invariants.is_empty(){eprintln!("security: audit --json lists paths, exclusions and grant writers; guide security explains proof scope");}
}
